```
//
// Half-LifeTests ObserveCutoffWarningUseCaseTests
//

import ComposableArchitecture
import Foundation
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks the cutoff warning's use case and registration: OBSWARN-1 and DEP-WARN in the Caffeine Cutoff article.
struct ObserveCutoffWarningUseCaseTests {

    static let latte = FavouriteDrink(type: .latte, quantity: 2)
    static let warning = CutoffWarning.stillRisingAtBedtime(bedtime: Date(timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate: 22.5 * 3_600))

    /// OBSWARN-1: it streams every warning the repository publishes for the drink, how long ago, and the calendar.
    @Test func streamsTheWarningsTheRepositoryPublishesForTheDrink() async throws {
        var tokyo = Calendar(identifier: .gregorian)
        tokyo.timeZone = try #require(TimeZone(identifier: "Asia/Tokyo"))
        let tokyoTimeZone = tokyo.timeZone
        let repository = FakeCaffeineDecayRepository(warnings: { drink, secondsAgo, calendar in
            drink == Self.latte && secondsAgo == 3_600 && calendar.timeZone == tokyoTimeZone ? [nil, Self.warning] : []
        })
        let observe = ObserveCutoffWarningUseCase(repository: repository)

        var received: [CutoffWarning?] = []
        let input = ObserveCutoffWarningUseCase.Input(drink: Self.latte, secondsAgo: 3_600, calendar: tokyo)
        for await warning in try await executeThroughProtocol(observe, input) {
            received.append(warning)
        }

        #expect(received == [nil, Self.warning])
    }

    /// DEP-WARN: a test that observes the warning without overriding the use case fails.
    @Test func testObserveCutoffWarningReportsAnIssue() async {
        await withKnownIssue {
            @Dependency(\.observeCutoffWarning) var observeCutoffWarning
            let input = ObserveCutoffWarningUseCase.Input(
                drink: Self.latte, secondsAgo: 0, calendar: Calendar(identifier: .gregorian))
            for await _ in observeCutoffWarning.execute(input) {}
        }
    }
}

extension SwiftDataStoreTests {

    /// Checks the cutoff warning's preview registration, which opens an empty in-memory store. It runs inside the
    /// serialized `SwiftDataStoreTests`, because it opens a store.
    @Suite(.timeLimit(.minutes(1)))
    struct CutoffWarningPreviewDependencyTests {

        /// DEP-WARN: observing the warning uses the one app-scoped decay repository.
        @Test func previewObserveCutoffWarningUsesTheAppScopedRepository() {
            withDependencies {
                $0.context = .preview
            } operation: {
                @Dependency(\.caffeineDecayRepository) var repository
                @Dependency(\.observeCutoffWarning) var observeCutoffWarning
                #expect((observeCutoffWarning.repository as AnyObject) === (repository as AnyObject))
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**2026-09-13 00:04:23 · Tool result**

```
272	## Presentation
273	
274	Presentation maps each `DrinkType` to its user-facing parts. The Domain holds none of them.
275	
276	- **Names and units** live in `Localizable.xcstrings` (constitution Article VII). Unit labels use plural variants ("1 shot", "2 shots"). One `LocalizedStringResource` per drink serves both the views and the `AppEnum`'s display names.
277	- **Caffeine amounts** are formatted with locale-aware APIs and rounded for display.
278	- **Icons** are described in "Iconography".
279	
280	### Iconography
281	
282	Every drink in the list and on the one-tap row gets an icon. The owner chose SF Symbols for now, because they're first-party. Custom symbols can replace them later without touching the Domain. The symbol set covers the drink families, but not each drink:
283	
284	| Drinks | SF Symbol |
285	|--------|-----------|
286	| Espresso, americano, latte, cappuccino, flat white | `cup.and.saucer.fill` |
287	| Drip coffee, instant coffee | `mug.fill` |
288	| Cold brew | `takeoutbag.and.cup.and.straw.fill` |
289	| Black tea, green tea | `cup.and.heat.waves.fill` |
290	| Matcha | `leaf.fill` |
291	| Energy drink | `bolt.fill` |
292	| Cola | `takeoutbag.and.cup.and.straw.fill`, shared with cold brew. No symbol shows a can. |
293	
294	Each icon sits next to the drink's name, so it's decorative and hidden from VoiceOver (constitution Article VI.1). The name carries the meaning, so shared icons don't make a drink ambiguous.
295	
296	### The composer screen
297	
298	The owner chose on 2026-09-11: one screen, opened as a sheet from the root's log button, closing after a successful Add.
299	
300	On 2026-09-12 the owner changed the layout: the drinks scroll sideways as tiles, and the panel always shows. The sheet is only as tall as its content, and the composer draws its own compact header, "Log a drink" with a Close button, instead of the system navigation bar.
301	
302	``DrinkComposerView`` shows a row of tiles that scrolls sideways, one per drink, each with its icon above its name. The chosen tile is dark, with a checkmark. Three tiles fill the row: two from the xLarge text size, and one at accessibility sizes. The row snaps to tile edges. Below the row, the chosen drink's panel always shows:
303	
304	- The drink's name and its estimate, such as "125 mg", from ``DrinkComposerFeature``'s `estimatedMilligrams`.
305	- The quantity with its unit, such as "2 shots", with round buttons that add or remove one. Choosing a drink starts its quantity at the drink's `defaultQuantity`, and it never goes below 1.
306	- The "When" choices: Now, 1h, 2h, and 4h ago. The durations are formatted for the locale (constitution Article VII.3).
307	- "Add 125 mg", which logs the drink through ``LogDrinkUseCase`` and closes the composer. If the drink can't be saved, the composer stays open and shows a short message, and the reducer logs the error's domain and code (Article XI.6.4). Close leaves without logging.
308	
309	A drink is always chosen. The composer opens on the last drink logged, meaning the most recently consumed one, with its quantity. It reads the log through `ObserveLoggedDrinksUseCase`. Until the log arrives, or when nothing has been logged, the composer shows one espresso shot. The log never replaces a drink or quantity the user has already chosen. The owner chose this on 2026-09-12.
310	
311	The one-tap row joined the composer on 2026-09-12 (<doc:OneTapLog>). It sits under the header in its slim style: each favourite's name, quantity, and caffeine, with no heading or icon, so the sheet stays under three quarters of the screen. A tap logs the favourite as consumed now and closes the composer, as Add does. At accessibility text sizes, the row moves under the panel, so the panel's controls stay on screen.
```

```sh
    private func cutoffWarningBanner(_ warning: CutoffWarning) -> some View {
        Label {
            VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: Spacing.sectionHeaderGap) {
                Text("After your cutoff")
                    .font(.footnote.weight(.semibold))
                cutoffWarningText(warning)

    }

    /// Why the drink is past its cutoff, in the wording the owner approved. The amount rounds up to the whole
    /// milligram, so it never shows at or under the threshold it's compared with.
    private func cutoffWarningText(_ warning: CutoffWarning) -> Text {
        switch warning {
        case let .tooMuchAtBedtime(level, threshold):
            let amount = CaffeineFormat.milligrams(level.milligrams.rounded(.up))
            let bedtime = level.date.formatted(date: .omitted, time: .shortened)
            let limit = CaffeineFormat.milligrams(threshold.milligrams)
            return Text(
                """
                About \\(amount) would still be in you at your \\(bedtime) bedtime. Sleep studies suggest \\
                under \\(limit).
                """)
        case let .stillRisingAtBedtime(bedtime):
            return Text("This would still be rising at your \\(bedtime.formatted(date: .omitted, time: .shortened)) bedtime.")
        }
    }

    private var addButton: some View {
'''),
]

def entry(key, comment):
    return f'    {json.dumps(key)} : {{\n      "comment" : {json.dumps(comment)}\n    }},\n'
new = [
    ("About %@ would still be in you at your %@ bedtime. Sleep studies suggest under %@.",
     "The drink composer's cutoff warning, when the chosen drink would leave too much caffeine at bedtime. The arguments are the amount left, such as \"51 mg\", the bedtime, such as \"10:30 PM\", and the threshold, such as \"40 mg\"."),
    ("After your cutoff",

     "The drink composer's cutoff warning, when the chosen drink wouldn't peak before bedtime. The argument is the bedtime, such as \"10:30 PM\"."),
]
import re
for key, comment in new:
    if f'    {json.dumps(key)} : {{' in s: sys.exit(f"already there: {key}")
    keys = [(m.start(), json.loads(m.group(1))) for m in re.finditer(r'^    ("(?:[^"\\]|\\.)*") : \{', s, re.M)]
    after = [pos for pos, k in keys if k.lower() > key.lower()]
    pos = after[0] if after else None
    if pos is None: sys.exit("no position")
    s = s[:pos] + entry(key, comment) + s[pos:]
json.loads(s)
open(c, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
print("view and catalog updated")
```

```sh
     "| WARN-3 | A drink consumed less than the peak delay before bedtime gets `stillRisingAtBedtime`, however small it is. |\n"

     "| WARN-5 | A drink at its own cutoff gets no warning, and the same drink half an hour later gets one. |\n"),

     "| WARNREPO-1 | `cutoffWarning(for:secondsAgo:in:)` gives a new subscriber the warning for the drink, consumed `secondsAgo` before the current time, from the same inputs as the cutoff, or `nil` when it fits. |\n"
     "| WARNREPO-2 | After a change to the drink log that alters it, the subscriber gets the new warning. |\n"

     "- **What it checks.** ``DrinkComposerFeature`` observes ``ObserveCutoffWarningUseCase`` for the chosen drink, quantity, and \"When\", in the calendar dependency. import sys
```

```sh
# 1. The banner as its own view.
banner = header + '''// Half-Life CutoffWarningBanner
//

import SwiftUI

/// The drink composer's cutoff warning: the heading, and why the chosen drink, at the time chosen, is past its caffeine
/// cutoff, in the wording the owner approved.
///
/// It sits above Add, which it never disables. VoiceOver reads it as one element. Its symbol is decorative, so the text
/// carries the meaning (constitution Article VI.1 and VI.3). See the Drink Composer and Caffeine Cutoff articles.
@MainActor
struct CutoffWarningBanner: View {
    /// Why the drink is past its cutoff.
    let warning: CutoffWarning

    /// The heading and the reason, in the caution colors, with a warning symbol.
    var body: some View {
        Label {
            VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: Spacing.sectionHeaderGap) {
                Text("After your cutoff")
                    .font(.footnote.weight(.semibold))
                reason
                    .font(.footnote)
            }
            .fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
        } icon: {
            Image(systemName: "exclamationmark.triangle.fill")
                .accessibilityHidden(true)
        }
        .foregroundStyle(Color.feedbackCaution)
        .padding(Spacing.itemGap)
        .frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)
        .background(
            Color.feedbackCautionBackground, in: RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: CornerRadius.small, style: .continuous)
        )
        .accessibilityElement(children: .combine)
        .accessibilityIdentifier(DrinkComposerViewAccessibilityID.cutoffWarning)
    }

    /// Why the drink is past its cutoff. The amount rounds up to the whole milligram, so it never shows at or under the
    /// threshold it's compared with.
    private var reason: Text {
        switch warning {
        case let .tooMuchAtBedtime(level, threshold):
            let amount = CaffeineFormat.milligrams(level.milligrams.rounded(.up))
            let bedtime = level.date.formatted(date: .omitted, time: .shortened)
            let limit = CaffeineFormat.milligrams(threshold.milligrams)
            return Text(
                """
                About \\(amount) would still be in you at your \\(bedtime) bedtime. Sleep studies suggest \\
                under \\(limit).
                """)
        case let .stillRisingAtBedtime(bedtime):
            let time = bedtime.formatted(date: .omitted, time: .shortened)
            return Text("This would still be rising at your \\(time) bedtime.")
        }
    }
}
'''
```

```
### 2026-09-12 23:01 -0400 — Build the cutoff reminder: a notification at the caffeine cutoff

- **Started:** 2026-09-12 23:01 -0400
- **Ended:** {END}
- **Human:** Adam Ure
- **AI tool / model:** Claude Code / Claude Opus 5
- **Transcript:** `ai_transcripts/2026-09-12-2258-2dd43bee.md` (not yet exported. The name is the exporter's, from the session's first timestamp, 22:58.)
- **Type:** change, planning
- **Request:** Send a local notification at the user's caffeine cutoff. Recalculate it when more caffeine is logged. When there's no caffeine in the body, assume the user's most frequent drink.
- **Interactions:**
  - `23:01` The owner asked for a local notification at the cutoff. It's recalculated when more caffeine is logged, and assumes the most frequent drink when there's no caffeine in the body.
  - `23:04` The owner chose to schedule the next seven nights. The wording names the drink and the bedtime, with no caffeine amount.
  - `23:07` The owner said notifications should be triggered from a data source. The time comes from the session transcript, because no clock was read then.
  - `23:10` The AI asked what that meant. The owner chose a view-less feature that sends the command through a use case and a repository to a data source. The alternative was a repository that reschedules itself.
  - `23:14` The owner also asked for the drink composer's cutoff warning, which is logged as the next entry. The time comes from the transcript. The AI's clock read 23:15.
  - `23:54` The owner reported an error, "no type called CutoffWarning", during the decay repository refactor (below). The time comes from the transcript.
- **AI contribution:**
  - **The math already existed.** `CaffeineCutoffRule` already sized the cutoff for the most-logged drink, and counted the caffeine already logged. With nothing logged, it uses the first starter. So the reminder reuses it.
  - **Tests first, then built:**
    - CUTOFF-9: `CaffeineCutoffRule.cutoffs(_:nights:now:calendar:)`. Each later night's cutoff is calculated from the moment the bedtime before it has passed. With nothing logged, every night's cutoff falls at the same time of day.
    - CUTREPO-5: `upcomingCutoffs(nights:in:)` on `CaffeineDecayRepository`.
    - **Domain:** `CutoffReminder`, `CutoffReminderRepository`, `ObserveUpcomingCutoffsUseCase`, and `ScheduleCutoffRemindersUseCase`.
    - **Data:**
      - `CutoffReminderDataSource`.
```

### 2026-09-13 00:26:32 · Claude

Both features are built and pass their tests. The full test run with coverage is still going, and I'll add the two `ai_log.md` entries once it finishes. The one thing I need from you is a decision on the notification's wording, below.

**Cutoff notification.** Each night at the cutoff, the app sends:
- **Title:** "Last cup"
- **Body:** "A Latte, 2 shots, now still clears by your 10:30 PM bedtime. After this, caffeine will still be in you when you go to bed."

**How it recalculates:**
- **Tonight's reminder** counts the drinks already logged, sized for the user's most-logged drink.
- **The next six nights** are scheduled too. Each assumes nothing more is drunk, which gives your "no caffeine in the system" case: the usual drink alone. So a reminder still fires on days the app isn't opened.
- **What triggers a reschedule:** logging or deleting a drink, a change of bedtime or half-life, tonight's cutoff passing, or notification permission changing.
- **What's skipped:** a night with no room left gets no reminder, and nothing is scheduled while notifications aren't allowed.
- **Design, as you chose it:** a feature with no screen, running under the app's root, sends each reschedule through a use case and a repository. Only the data source touches `UNUserNotificationCenter`.
- **The math already existed:** the "Last cup" tile's cutoff rule. The notification reuses it.

**Composer warning.** When the chosen drink, amount and time break the cutoff, a caution banner appears above Add. Add stays enabled. The wording is the one you approved at 23:28:
- **Too much at bedtime:** "After your cutoff. About 51 mg would still be in you at your 10:30 PM bedtime. Sleep studies suggest under 40 mg."
- **Too close to bedtime to peak first:** "This would still be rising at your 10:30 PM bedtime."
- **A choice I made without asking:** the warning checks the exact amount at bedtime, not the tile's half-hour rounding. With the rounding, it could warn about 40 mg while quoting an amount under 40. The catch is that a drink a few minutes past the tile's "By 1:00 PM" may not warn.

**Results so far:**
- **Unit tests:** every suite I touched passes.
- **UI tests:** the warning appears for a large drink at any time of day, and Add still logs it. The composer also passes the accessibility audit with the warning showing.
- **Lint and docs:** swift-format and SwiftLint are clean on all my files, and the DocC build produced no warnings from the app's catalog.
- **Not yet verified:** the full run and the coverage number. My earlier full run couldn't compile because of another session's in-progress Settings change.
- **Can't be tested automatically:** a UI test can't see pending notifications. That a reminder actually appears at the cutoff has to be checked by hand on a device.

**Decision for you: the notification's wording.** "Still clears" and "caffeine will still be in you" say more than the app knows. A cup at the cutoff can leave up to 40 mg at bedtime, and some caffeine is always left. A more careful version: "…now still fits before your 10:30 PM bedtime. After this, more caffeine than sleep studies suggest would be in you at bedtime." Also, the body shows the drink's name capitalized ("A Latte"), where your preview had "a latte". Lower-casing names isn't correct in every language.

**Worth knowing:**
- **Only while the app is running:** rescheduling happens while the app is running.
- **Tapping the notification** opens the app where it was, not the composer.
- **Roadmap order:** both features were built ahead of roadmap ranks 10 to 18.

**Your "no type called CutoffWarning" error** came from one of my scripted edits failing halfway. For a few minutes the project had a new requirement that one test stand-in didn't meet yet. I fixed it within minutes.

**The decay-repository split.** Our two sessions' changes pushed `LiveCaffeineDecayRepository` over SwiftLint's size limits. At half-life-1c's request, I restructured it, and 1c's sleep-window tests still pass.

Nothing is committed.
